**Topic 1 Decisions**
The first `#askQuestions` round established:

- **Loop ownership:** Jetson runs whole-body control and `ros2_control`; STM32 schedules buses and enforces watchdogs/limits; motors close FOC/MIT.
- **Command contract:** Full MIT tuple: position, velocity, $K_p$, $K_d$, and feed-forward torque.
- **Firmware migration:** Versioned protocol redesign with capability negotiation and a migration path.
- **Planning envelope:** Approximately 14 joints: 4×RS03, 5×RS02, 5×RS00, targeting 200–500 Hz.
- **Future fieldbus:** Deliberately undecided. CAN, CAN-FD gateways, and EtherCAT must remain backend choices rather than assumptions in locomotion or AI code.

Our working invariant is therefore:

> Higher layers depend only on stable logical joint and sensor interfaces. Changing the physical transport may change the selected hardware plugin and deployment configuration, but not perception, behavior, locomotion, or whole-body controller code.

A `ros2_control` System hardware component is the appropriate initial abstraction because it represents coordinated multi-joint hardware and participates directly in the controller manager’s `read → update → write` loop. Jazzy also supports separate hardware `rw_rate` values, which matters because the present STM32 SPI exchange provides fresh actuator data at only 200 Hz. A 500 Hz controller loop would otherwise reuse state and decimate writes unless that firmware rate is raised.

**Verified Caveat**
We should not treat “CycloneDDS + Iceoryx” as an automatic end-to-end zero-copy guarantee. Current Jazzy documentation lists `rmw_cyclonedds` loaned messages as unsupported, while the Jazzy source contains conditional support when CycloneDDS is built with shared memory enabled. That path is limited to fixed-size/self-contained messages and compatible QoS. We will treat SHM as a measured deployment optimization and require an on-target cross-container test.

The timing calculation rules out a single RobStride bus for the target. With one 8-byte extended command and one 8-byte feedback frame per motor per cycle, a conservative stuffed-frame budget is about 160 bits per frame: four motors consume roughly 64% of a 1 Mbps branch at 500 Hz, while five approach 80% before retries or maintenance traffic. The target therefore needs at least four independently scheduled classic-CAN branches for 14 motors.

**MIT Contract**

`ros2_control` does not need a custom structured interface type. Each joint exports five scalar command interfaces:

| Interface | Unit | Meaning |
|---|---:|---|
| `<joint>/position` | rad | Desired joint position $q_d$ |
| `<joint>/velocity` | rad/s | Desired joint velocity $\dot q_d$ |
| `<joint>/effort` | N·m | Feed-forward torque $\tau_{ff}$ |
| `<joint>/stiffness` | N·m/rad | MIT proportional gain $K_p$ |
| `<joint>/damping` | N·m·s/rad | MIT derivative gain $K_d$ |

The drive-side command remains:

$$
\tau = K_p(q_d-q) + K_d(\dot q_d-\dot q) + \tau_{ff}
$$

`MitImpedanceController` must claim all five interfaces for every controlled joint. Partial command-mode starts, stops, or ownership changes are rejected in `prepare_command_mode_switch()`. No generic position, velocity, or effort controller may own a subset.

During each update, the controller writes every tuple from one preallocated command set. `HumanoidActuatorSystem::write()` then snapshots all five values for all joints, assigns one cycle sequence number, validates the complete set, and passes that immutable snapshot to the selected transport.

Mandatory state interfaces are `position`, `velocity`, and `effort`. Custom `valid`, `sample_age`, and `faulted` scalars let the controller reject stale data. Detailed timestamps, sequence numbers, and `uint32_t` fault masks stay in the real-time snapshot and are copied to non-real-time diagnostics.

**Hardware Layers**

1. `HumanoidActuatorSystem` owns ROS names, joint-side SI units, lifecycle behavior, hard limits, freshness rules, and transport-independent snapshots.
2. `ActuatorTransport` has `Stm32SerialTransport`, `EthercatTransport`, `SocketCanTransport` for benches, and simulation/replay implementations.
3. Model codecs own direction, offset, gear ratio, torque conversion, and RS00/RS02/RS03 wire scaling. CAN IDs never escape this layer.
4. A schema-validated manifest derived from configs/slave0.yaml generates host tables, gateway tables, EtherCAT mappings, and a shared configuration hash.

Activation performs discovery and capability negotiation, verifies protocol and configuration hashes, obtains a complete recent state sample, and seeds a safe command. Deactivation and errors transition through `DISABLED → ARMED → ACTIVE → SAFE_DAMPING / FAULT_LATCHED`.

**Timing Policy**

| Stage | Controller Manager | Actuator I/O | Rule |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Current robot | 200 Hz | 200 Hz | Genuine fresh 5 ms samples |
| Production target | 500 Hz | 500 Hz | Genuine fresh 2 ms samples |
| Slower sensors | Independent | `rw_rate` allowed | Separate hardware component |

A permanent 500 Hz controller over 200 Hz actuator I/O is rejected. Jazzy’s `rw_rate` decimates `read()` and `write()` calls; it cannot create fresh measurements or commands.

In EtherCAT cycle $N$, gateways latch command sequence $N$, complete their local CAN exchange, and return feedback tagged $N$ in cycle $N+1$. This is a defined one-cycle control latency, not stale-data reuse.

**Physical Topology**

Use a dedicated native or PCIe Ethernet interface, an EtherCAT line of two-port gateways, and Distributed Clocks for synchronized command latching. EtherCAT must not share a NIC with Zenoh, ZED traffic, SSH, or ordinary IP networking. A production proof should prioritize IgH EtherCAT Master 1.6; SOEM remains useful for initial gateway bring-up.

For an eight-byte extended command and feedback frame, reserve approximately 160 wire bits per frame:

$$
U \approx \frac{n \cdot 2 \cdot 500 \cdot 160}{10^6}
$$

Four motors use about 64% of a 1 Mbps branch; five approach 80% before retries, diagnostics, and error frames. Therefore four isolated branches arranged approximately 4/4/3/3 are the planning baseline. CAN-FD-capable gateways are acceptable, but the RobStride endpoints remain classic CAN and receive no bitrate-switching benefit.

**Cyclic Protocol**

Each fixed-size gateway command PDO carries protocol version, cycle sequence, apply deadline, valid mask, and five finite `float32` SI values per joint. The state PDO carries echoed sequence, Distributed Clock timestamp, complete-sample mask, measured state, temperature, and fault summary.

Mailbox configuration exchanges firmware build identity, supported rate, channel and model capabilities, wire ranges, and configuration hash. A mismatch prevents transition to `ACTIVE`. The interim serial protocol should carry the same semantics with explicit serialization, COBS framing, sequence/deadline fields, and CRC32C.

**Safety Ownership**

| Layer | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| Controller | Soft limits, torque/gain slew limits, reference freshness, controlled saturation |
| SystemInterface | Finite checks, complete tuples, hard joint/model envelopes, sequence and state-age validation |
| Gateway/drive | Deadline watchdog, bus-off and thermal handling, electrical limits, safe damping, eventual disable |
| Independent hardware | Hardwired E-stop and actuator-enable or power interruption |

A hard-envelope violation rejects the whole command cycle; fields are not independently clamped into a different control law. Missing deadlines trigger local safe damping with $K_p=0$, $\dot q_d=0$, $\tau_{ff}=0$, and validated $K_{d,\mathrm{safe}}$. Persistent faults disable the drives and require explicit re-arming.

**Qualification Gates**

1. Run the existing transport at measured 200 Hz with sequence, age, watchdog, and configuration instrumentation.
2. Qualify one four-motor CAN branch at 500 Hz, including worst-case stuffing, bus errors, and thermal load.
3. Qualify four EtherCAT gateways with all 14 joints and feedback sequence $N$ returned by cycle $N+1$.
4. Require zero hidden sample reuse, zero mixed-cycle snapshots, and zero actuator deadline misses during an eight-hour fully loaded HIL test.
5. Inject Jetson loss, EtherCAT loss, CAN bus-off, stale sequence, malformed tuples, and configuration mismatch; each must reach the specified safe state.
6. Swap `stm32_serial` for `ethercat` using configuration only. The WBC, MIT controller, and higher-level nodes must use the same binaries and interfaces.

RS03 MIT wire scaling remains a mandatory bench-verification item before torque-enabled operation. Topic 1 is otherwise frozen and ready to become a normative section of the final architecture document. No workspace files were changed.

The architecture is now narrowed to four authorities: a tuple-aware safety kernel inside `HumanoidActuatorSystem`, an independent STM32 safety agent, a non-real-time lifecycle coordinator, and a hardwired stop chain. One correction to the earlier baseline is important: current per-motor torque-trip behavior must not simply drop one supporting joint to `IDLE`; for a humanoid, that can be more hazardous than a coordinated whole-robot protective response.

```
Q: Approve a hardwired, de-energize-to-stop actuator-power chain independent of Jetson, ROS, USB, and STM32 application logic?
A: Approve independent power cut - Mandatory physical stop chain; remote emergency input may be added as an independent channel.
Q: What should the initial response be when any actuator reports a protective or critical fault?
A: Isolate failed joint - Disable only the reporting joint and continue the rest of the robot.
Q: Approve local safe damping followed by disable when fresh host commands stop?
A: Damp, then disable - STM32 commands zero feed-forward/stiffness with validated damping, then disables after a bounded interval.
Q: Approve explicit rearming with stricter reset requirements for emergency and critical faults?
A: Tiered explicit rearm - E-stop/critical faults need physical reset plus software arm; transient faults still return DISARMED and require a fresh arm handshake.
```

Analyzing your answers...

Your selections establish the core behavior: physical emergency removal, per-joint immediate containment, local damping on command loss, and no automatic reactivation. I’m resolving one consequence before freezing the proposal: “isolate the joint” can be guaranteed only if the drive acknowledges disable or has an independent power domain, and it must not silently authorize the WBC to continue a gait that still assumes the missing joint.

```
Q: After a joint is isolated, when may the remaining actuators continue active control?
A: Only validated degraded modes - Controller must acknowledge the new availability mask and select a prequalified policy; otherwise remaining joints damp then disable.
Q: If a failed drive does not acknowledge disable or still produces motion/torque, approve escalation to the hard actuator-power cut?
A: Escalate to power cut - Required while the current robot lacks independently monitored per-joint power isolation.
```

**Topic 1 Amendment**

The production baseline is now:

- `HumanoidActuatorSystem`
- `Stm32SerialTransport`
- Genuine 200 Hz command and feedback exchange
- No committed successor bus or topology

Future backends must satisfy the same safety contract, but EtherCAT, CAN-FD, multi-CAN, and other physical designs remain undecided.

# Topic 2: Safety and Fault Management

## Safety Architecture

```mermaid
flowchart TB
    WBC["WBC / MIT Controller"] --> SK["SafetyKernel<br/>hard command gate"]
    SC["SafetyCoordinator<br/>lifecycle and recovery"] -->|"RT-safe intent mailbox"| SK

    SK --> AT["ActuatorTransport"]
    AT --> ESA["Embedded Safety Agent<br/>current: Master + Slave STM32"]
    ESA --> DR["RobStride Drives"]

    DR --> ESA
    ESA --> AT
    AT --> SK

    GC["GameController emergency state"] --> SC
    IMU["Future fall / power monitors"] --> SC

    ES["Onboard E-stop<br/>optional independent remote E-stop"] --> PS["Latching actuator-power stop chain"]
    ESA -->|"stop request + hardware heartbeat"| PS
    PS --> DR

    SK -.-> OBS["Non-RT fault telemetry<br/>Zenoh / logs"]
```

The physical stop chain bypasses Jetson, ROS, Zenoh, USB, and STM32 application logic. It removes actuator power while retaining logic power for diagnostics. Releasing or resetting an E-stop never restarts motion.

## Safety Authorities

| Layer | Authority |
|---|---|
| WBC/MIT controller | Smooth soft limits, collision constraints, command and gain slew limits |
| `SafetyKernel` | Final atomic MIT-tuple validation, hard envelopes, freshness, fault policy |
| Embedded Safety Agent | Local watchdogs, joint isolation, safe damping, drive faults, overload protection |
| RobStride drive | Internal electrical, thermal, encoder, and FOC protection |
| Hardwired stop chain | Final removal of actuator energy |
| `SafetyCoordinator` | Arming, recovery, degraded-mode selection, operator and GameController requests |

ROS lifecycle state is orchestration state, not safety truth. Safety remains effective if the controller manager crashes.

## Safety State Machine

```mermaid
stateDiagram-v2
    [*] --> POWER_REMOVED
    POWER_REMOVED --> DISARMED: physical reset
    DISARMED --> ARMING_CHECKS: explicit arm request
    ARMING_CHECKS --> ACTIVE_NOMINAL: all checks pass

    ACTIVE_NOMINAL --> CONTAINING: protective joint fault
    CONTAINING --> DEGRADED_ACTIVE: isolation verified and policy approved
    CONTAINING --> SAFE_DAMPING: no approved policy or timeout
    DEGRADED_ACTIVE --> SAFE_DAMPING: additional fault
    SAFE_DAMPING --> DISABLED_LATCHED: damping interval complete

    ACTIVE_NOMINAL --> SAFE_DAMPING: host or transport command loss
    ACTIVE_NOMINAL --> POWER_REMOVED: E-stop or uncontrolled motion
    CONTAINING --> POWER_REMOVED: isolation not verified

    DISABLED_LATCHED --> DISARMED: fault stable and explicit reset
```

A failed joint is isolated first. The other joints may resume active control only after the controller acknowledges a new availability mask and selects a prequalified degraded policy. Until Topic 3 validates such policies, the table is empty: the remaining joints safely damp and disable after isolation.

## Fault Policy

| Class | Examples | Required response |
|---|---|---|
| Advisory | Rising temperature, occasional CRC error, timing margin reduced | Report, apply hysteresis, optionally derate |
| Joint protective | Overheat, driver fault, encoder fault, stall, hard-limit violation | Isolate joint and verify inactive state |
| System protective | Stale host commands, serial/SPI loss, invalid tuple, widespread undervoltage | Safe-damp all joints, then disable |
| Critical/emergency | Physical E-stop, uncontrolled motion, power-interlock mismatch, failed isolation | Remove actuator power and latch |

The RobStride `undervoltage`, `driver_fault`, `overheat`, `encoder_fault`, `stall_overload`, and `uncalibrated` bits map into canonical transport-independent fault codes. Backend-specific codes remain attached as diagnostic detail.

## Safety Envelope

The final gate evaluates the complete MIT command, including its resulting torque:

$$
\tau_{\mathrm{est}}
=K_p(q_d-q)+K_d(\dot q_d-\dot q)+\tau_{ff}
$$

The permitted dynamic torque is:

$$
\tau_{\mathrm{allowed}}
=\min\left(
\tau_{\mathrm{joint}},
\tau_{\mathrm{motor}}(T,\text{overload history}),
\tau_{\mathrm{electrical}},
\tau_{\mathrm{operating}}
\right)
$$

Every cycle validates:

- All five MIT fields are finite and belong to one sequence.
- Feedback is fresh enough to evaluate the command safely.
- Position, velocity, gains, feed-forward torque, estimated total torque, tracking error, and slew rates are bounded.
- Commands near a joint limit cannot drive farther into the limit.
- Continuous and temporary torque use a conservative overload budget based on the supplied model-specific curves.
- Physical motor limits are distinct from RobStride wire-encoding ranges.

Soft violations are shaped smoothly by the controller. A hard violation rejects the entire tuple and invokes the configured protective action; fields are never independently clamped into a different impedance law.

## Shared Safety Manifest

One reviewed manifest generates independent Jetson and STM32 representations. Arming requires matching version and hash.

It contains joint mechanical limits, motor model, calibration identity, maximum gains and slew rates, continuous/peak torque curves, temperature thresholds, watchdog periods, safe-damping profile, isolation timeout, fault policy, and approved degraded-mode masks.

Safety-critical limits cannot be loosened while armed. Host and MCU validators are implemented independently and tested against shared conformance vectors to reduce common-mode defects.

## Transport Safety Contract

Every production `ActuatorTransport` must provide equivalent semantics:

```text
SafeCommandFrame:
  session_epoch, sequence, lifetime_cycles
  active_joint_mask, safety_action
  complete MIT tuples

StateFrame:
  echoed_sequence, fresh_mask, active_mask, isolated_mask
  canonical_faults, raw_faults, temperatures
  command_applied / isolation_verified
```

Required abstract actions are `APPLY`, `SAFE_DAMP`, `ISOLATE`, `DISABLE`, and `REQUEST_POWER_CUT`. Capability negotiation must reject activation if a backend cannot provide the watchdog, isolation, or acknowledgement semantics required by the manifest.

## STM32 Production Baseline

For `Stm32SerialTransport`:

1. The Master watchdog is refreshed only by a complete, valid, advancing host command, not by arbitrary serial traffic or a ping.
2. The Slave has an independent SPI-command watchdog.
3. Each motor has a feedback-age watchdog.
4. Both MCUs boot with motor enable deasserted and stale session epochs invalidated.
5. Command loss applies a firmware-resident profile: $q_d=q$, $\dot q_d=0$, $K_p=0$, $\tau_{ff}=0$, and validated $K_{d,\mathrm{safe}}$, followed by disable.
6. A joint fault suppresses further MIT frames and requests RobStride Type 4 disable.
7. Isolation must be verified from drive state and telemetry. Missing feedback, continued torque, or an unacknowledged disable escalates to actuator-power removal.
8. An external hardware watchdog or safety relay monitors the embedded enable/heartbeat path so a stalled MCU cannot leave power enabled indefinitely.

The serial and SPI protocols therefore need session epochs, sequence numbers, configuration hashes, command lifetimes, complete masks, CRC protection, and explicit safety acknowledgements.

## ROS Integration

`HumanoidActuatorSystem` executes the local safety action before returning `ERROR`. Jazzy will then deactivate controllers using the failed hardware, but that behavior is secondary and is not relied upon to stop motion.

Validated degraded operation keeps the hardware component alive while exposing an availability mask. The remainder stays in safe damping until a compatible controller explicitly acknowledges that mask. Generic `ros2_control` scalar clamping is insufficient for the composite MIT torque equation; the custom tuple-aware gate remains authoritative.

## Rearming

Arming requires released and reset physical stops, correct contactor feedback, matching protocol and manifest hashes, valid calibration, all required joints discovered, fresh stationary telemetry, no active critical faults, and an explicit time-limited arm request.

Transient and protective faults require stable recovery plus software rearm. E-stop and critical faults require physical reset, operator acknowledgement, and software arm. Every recovery creates a new session epoch, so delayed pre-fault commands cannot execute.

## Verification

The safety qualification suite must inject host death, frozen Master and Slave firmware, corrupt/duplicate/stale frames, every RobStride fault bit, lost motor feedback, invalid MIT tuples, torque and thermal overload, hard-limit violations, configuration mismatch, GameController emergency state, E-stop, and failed isolation.

Acceptance is based on measured detection, isolation, damping, disable, and power-removal latency using physical instrumentation, not ROS logs alone. The same fault-injection contract applies to every future transport backend.

This follows the error behavior documented by [ros2_control hardware components](https://control.ros.org/jazzy/doc/ros2_control/hardware_interface/doc/hardware_components_userdoc.html), the risk-reduction method of [ISO 12100](https://www.iso.org/standard/51528.html), the emergency-stop principles of [ISO 13850](https://www.iso.org/standard/59970.html), and the current [Humanoid League physical-stop requirements](https://github.com/RoboCup-HumanoidSoccerLeague/HSL-Rules/blob/master/rules/robot_players.tex). It is a competition/R&D safety baseline, not a claim of certified functional safety.


User: Topic 2 is approved with the following amendments, to be integrated before it is frozen:

1. **Fall management:** Falling is the primary routine hazard for a humanoid. Add fall detection and protective response as first-class safety concerns: define trigger sources and a detection→response latency budget in the safety manifest now (estimation details arrive in Topic 4), add post-fall states and rearm conditions to the state machine, and specify a protective brace/damp action for imminent falls that is distinct from command-loss damping.
2. **Numeric budgets:** Add normative worst-case latency targets (fault detection→containment, command loss→safe damping applied at drives, isolation timeout, damping duration before disable, power-cut) plus the physical measurement method for each. Measured latency without targets is not acceptance criteria.
3. **Ownership backstops:** Explicitly assign self-collision prevention (Topic 3 WBC primary — state what the safety kernel enforces as backstop), and classify Jetson thermal throttling, logic-power brownout, and battery faults (SOC/IR/undervoltage) in the fault policy.
4. **Startup interlock:** Specify arming preconditions for calibration identity, joint-state initialization, and entry into MIT mode (homing, initial q capture).
5. Keep the transport-agnostic contract and STM32 baseline exactly as proposed.

The four research lanes are back. The evidence is already converging on a useful separation: gait generation, whole-body feasibility, and joint impedance execution should be distinct contracts; learned policies are candidates behind the same motion-command boundary, not a parallel actuator path; and URDF/USD/MJCF should be generated views rather than three editable robot truths. I’m checking the strongest claims and incompatibilities now before turning that into a concrete Topic 3 proposal.

No matches found.

The key design tension is now explicit. A pure torque WBC would waste the motor-side impedance loop, while independently adding drive PD to a torque WBC can double-count feedback. I’m validating a tuple-aware inverse-dynamics formulation in which the QP reasons about the exact MIT law, plus the practical evidence for DCM-first planning, asynchronous MPC, and dual-simulator qualification.

Compacted conversationThe final support check changes one deployment assumption: Isaac Lab is a workstation or cloud training environment, not something the Orin Nano should be expected to host. Onboard deployment is the exported policy plus its signed observation/action manifest; MuJoCo remains the lightweight independent oracle and regression simulator, while neither simulator is allowed to become the editable source of physical truth.

Starting: *Draft Topic 3 proposal* (4/6)

Starting: *Collect Topic 3 decisions* (5/6)

**Topic 3 Proposal: Locomotion And Motion Control**

**Status:** proposed for review, not frozen. The accepted actuation, safety, and middleware decisions remain unchanged; no documentation files have been edited.

**Evidence Reconciliation**

- The earlier Pinocchio 3.9 claim is stale. Upstream is currently 4.1.0; the Jazzy status page reports 4.1.0 in build/testing and 4.0.0-2 in main. Production must pin an exact package, container digest, compiler, and solver combination.
- Isaac Lab 3.0 targets Isaac Sim 6.0/6.0.1. Its AArch64 guidance names systems such as DGX Spark, not Jetson Orin Nano. Isaac training therefore runs on a qualified workstation or cluster; the robot receives only exported policy artifacts.
- Published controller frequencies and solve times are evidence from specific robots, not guarantees for this robot. Every rate below remains subject to target-compute qualification.

**Paradigm Comparison**

| Paradigm | Strengths | Main limitations | Proposed role |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Scripted/keyframe | Deterministic, inspectable, quick to bootstrap | Brittle to disturbances and morphology changes | Calibration, initial get-up, brace, kicks, test motions |
| ZMP/LIPM/DCM preview | Mature, inexpensive, explainable, supports footstep adaptation | Simplified CoM/contact assumptions; limited full-body dynamics | Initial online walking reference generator |
| Centroidal MPC | Predicts contact forces, momentum, and footsteps | Model-sensitive; nonlinear solve time and stale-plan risks | Optional asynchronous planner after profiling |
| Inverse-dynamics QP/WBC | Enforces dynamics, contacts, torque limits, and competing tasks | Requires accurate state/model; solver failure must be contained | Mandatory 200 Hz whole-body realization candidate |
| End-to-end RL | Captures nonlinear behavior; inexpensive inference | Opaque distribution shift and large sim-to-real burden | Research lane only; no initial actuator authority |
| Residual/reference learning | Preserves deterministic structure while adapting model errors | Residual still needs feasibility and runtime bounds | First learned production candidate |
| Offline optimization/imitation | Strong for kicks, recovery, and teacher generation | Cannot react online without another controller | Primitive authoring and policy training |

These approaches are complementary layers, not one winner.

**Recommended Architecture**

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    B[Behavior intent] --> A[Motion supervisor and arbiter]
    S[SafetyCoordinator directive] --> A
    P[Stand walk stop kick get-up recovery] --> A
    L[Bounded learned proposer] --> R
    A --> R[DCM planner or optional centroidal MPC]
    A --> C[Contact-mode manager]
    R --> X[Depth-one RT reference buffer]
    E[Coherent state estimate] --> C
    E --> W[200 Hz inverse-dynamics WBC]
    C --> W
    X --> W
    W --> T[Single MIT tuple synthesizer]
    T --> K[SafetyKernel]
    K --> H[HumanoidActuatorSystem]
```

`MotionReference` is an immutable, versioned snapshot containing timestamps and validity, model hash, primitive and policy IDs, availability and contact epochs, planned contacts and wrench bounds, CoM/base/momentum references, foot trajectories, posture references, transition state, and abort target.

The contact-mode manager alone commits support changes. A planned touchdown is intent, not evidence; measured contact must confirm it. Missed touchdown triggers a qualified extension, replan, or stop rather than creating a fictitious rigid constraint. WBC problem structures are prebuilt for each supported contact mode.

**Whole-Body And MIT Realization**

The WBC enforces floating-base dynamics and committed contacts:

$$
M(q)\dot v+h(q,v)=S^\top\tau^\star+J_c(q)^\top\lambda,
\qquad
J_c\dot v+\dot J_c v=0
$$

Three realizations are relevant:

- **Pure torque:** $\tau_{ff}=\tau^\star,\ K_p=K_d=0$. It is mathematically clean but discards useful motor-side between-cycle impedance.
- **Model feed-forward plus impedance:** $\tau_{ff}=\tau_{\mathrm{ID,ff}}$. This is valid only if the WBC excludes the same joint-feedback terms and constrains the predicted total MIT torque.
- **Recommended initial realization:** WBC computes desired total torque $\tau^\star$ and references; the tuple synthesizer uses an approved gain profile and back-solves

$$
\tau_{ff}=\tau^\star-K_p(q_d-\hat q_a)-K_d(\dot q_d-\hat{\dot q}_a)
$$

where $(\hat q_a,\hat{\dot q}_a)$ is the state predicted for command application time. Thus the expected MIT torque equals $\tau^\star$ at that state, while motor PD supplies local impedance between 5 ms updates. The synthesizer rejects tuples whose residuals, feed-forward term, or uncertainty envelope are infeasible; it never clips the five fields independently.

Feedback ownership is explicit: WBC owns coupled whole-body feedback, motor gains own declared local impedance, the tuple synthesizer prevents instantaneous duplication, and `SafetyKernel` remains the final safety authority.

**Real-Time And Compute Profiles**

The controller performs one nonblocking reference read, contact arbitration, WBC solve, tuple synthesis, and safety evaluation per cycle:

$$
T_{\mathrm{read}}+T_{\mathrm{update}}+T_{\mathrm{write}}\leq5\text{ ms}
$$

| Profile | Enabled capability | Intended qualification target |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `BASELINE` | Deterministic DCM/LIPM planning and fixed-structure 200 Hz WBC | Orin Nano 8 GB |
| `PREDICTIVE` | Baseline plus asynchronous centroidal MPC | Any platform passing thermal and concurrent-load tests |
| `LEARNED_GUIDANCE` | Bounded footstep, gait, or task-space proposals with deterministic fallback | Separately qualified CPU/GPU configuration |

The RT path has no heap allocation, blocking lock, ROS/Zenoh wait, logging, model construction, JIT compilation, or TensorRT engine build. Model and solver workspaces are preallocated; planner and policy outputs use timestamped depth-one buffers. TensorRT engines are built, hashed, and benchmarked per target because they are not generally portable across GPU and software configurations.

Production RT code should be C++; model generation and training may use Python. Pinocchio is the proposed dynamics backend. TSID versus a project-owned Pinocchio/ProxQP formulation, and Crocoddyl versus OCS2 for later MPC, remain benchmark decisions rather than architecture commitments.

**Primitives And Degraded Operation**

Every primitive declares admission conditions, entry and exit contacts, priority, safe preemption points, freshness, timeout, completion criteria, and abort destination. Normal handover blends task-space references; only safety-directed stop or brace may preempt immediately.

A new availability epoch invalidates the active primitive. Continued motion requires an exact prequalified tuple of fault class, availability mask, contact mode, model variant, controller policy ID, and gain profile. Removing a joint from the QP dynamically is not a degraded-control policy. Missing or mismatched qualification falls back to the containment rules in ADR-002.

**Generated Model Contract**

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    C[CAD and identified physical data] --> M[Typed PhysicalModel bundle]
    Q[Per-robot calibration overlay] --> M
    M --> G[Validated deterministic generator]
    G --> U[URDF and semantic model]
    G --> J[MJCF]
    G --> D[USD]
    G --> X[Joint frame actuator and policy manifests]
    O[Versioned engine numerical overlays] --> J
    O --> D
```

The physical bundle owns SI units, hierarchy, frames, joint sign/zero/order, limits, full inertias and uncertainty, rotor/transmission behavior, friction/backlash, actuator saturation and delay, collision/contact geometry, sensors, and calibration provenance. Generated files are read-only. Simulator overlays may select integrators and contact-solver numerics but may not silently redefine physical truth.

**Qualification Gates**

1. Schema, units, frame, inertia, ordering, and generated-artifact checks.
2. FK, Jacobian, CoM, mass-matrix, gravity, and inverse-dynamics comparisons across random states.
3. Actuator-rig identification for delay, saturation, friction, impedance, and thermal behavior.
4. Identical tuple replay and perturbation tests in MuJoCo and Isaac Lab with declared tolerances.
5. Target-compute replay under worst-case perception load, clocks, power mode, and thermal steady state.
6. Passive HIL, then tethered low-energy energized HIL.
7. Primitive-specific dynamic qualification before field or competition use.

The robot-model gates through target-compute replay are hard blockers for dynamic hardware locomotion.

**Decisions Requested**

1. Approve the layered architecture and single MIT tuple owner.
2. Approve deterministic DCM/LIPM walking first, with centroidal MPC added asynchronously after profiling.
3. Approve torque-matched impedance as the initial WBC-to-MIT mapping.
4. Approve learned authority beginning with bounded footsteps, gait parameters, or task-space residuals.
5. Approve the typed physical bundle with generated URDF, MJCF, USD, and mapping manifests.
6. Approve capability-based compute profiles and offboard Isaac Lab training.
7. Approve the primitive, contact-transition, and exact degraded-policy contracts.
8. Defer concrete QP and MPC solver selection to reproducible Orin and workstation benchmarks.

Reply with `approve all` or amendments keyed to decisions `1` through `8`.

**Step 1 — remaining #askQuestions (before proposing; do not guess):**
- Kinematic layout: how are the 14 joints distributed across legs/arms/torso, which motor models go where, per-limb DOF, and are there passive or parallel mechanisms? This blocks model authoring.

**Step 2 — after my answers, deliver the Topic 3 architecture proposal** covering:

1. **Model single source of truth and quality gates.** URDF/Xacro organization and the generation flow into ros2_control configs, the controller/policy layer, both simulators, and the ADR-002 safety manifest (MIT wire scaling, calibration identity). Given near-zero model data, define the model-quality-gate ladder (kinematic correctness → geometric/mass properties → actuator torque-speed and friction characterization → contact parameters → sim-to-real correlation metrics) and the concrete measurement/identification campaign required to pass each gate. No dynamic locomotion before its gate passes.
2. **Paradigm-agnostic execution architecture.** Define the shared, candidate-independent infrastructure (RT skeleton, reference/state interface, primitive manager, sim harness, logging/evaluation) and the integration contract each candidate satisfies to plug in above the frozen Topic 1 boundary. Then define the **benchmark and downselect protocol**: metrics (walking speed and robustness to pushes, kick quality, get-up success rate, compute headroom on the minimum profile, sim-to-real gap), test progression (SIL → tethered rig → untethered), scoring method, and the decision gate and deadline. Parallel candidates must have an endpoint.
3. **Dual-simulator contract.** Assign Isaac Lab and MuJoCo roles, define the single generated model feeding both, cross-validation requirements, and confirm the simulator implements the same ActuatorTransport/SystemInterface five-tuple contract so identical controller binaries run in SIL and on hardware (per ADR-001's transport conformance suite).
4. **Execution model and RT boundary.** Chained ros2_control controllers vs a dedicated RT thread with lock-free buffers — decide and justify. Define the non-RT → RT reference flow (Zenoh → bounded timestamped buffer → 200 Hz loop) with latency/jitter budgets, honoring ADR-003's exclusion of middleware from the RT path.
5. **MIT tuple mapping per candidate.** For each paradigm: who computes τ_ff vs Kp/Kd (inverse dynamics/QP vs task-space impedance mapping vs learned gains), gain scheduling, and preservation of all-or-none tuple semantics and ADR-002's coupled envelope. Record the language policy explicitly (e.g., C++20 for the RT path, Python for training/tooling).
6. **Motion primitives and switching.** Stand/walk/kick/get-up/self-right primitives; switching under ADR-002 arming interlocks and availability-mask epochs; and the concrete interface through which prequalified degraded-mode policies are approved — this fills ADR-002's currently empty degraded-mode table.
7. **Compute and latency budgets per deployment profile.** Define named profiles (minimum: Orin Nano 8 GB; targets: Orin NX 16 GB / AGX Orin), per-stage worst-case budgets for each candidate on each profile, core pinning/isolation consistent with ADR-001/002 deadlines, and the headroom rule for profile promotion. Note what Topic 9 will later own for formalizing profiles.

"pdftotext=$($pdftotext.Source)"
foreach ($paper in $papers) {
  $pdf = Join-Path $env:TEMP "$($paper.Name).pdf"
  $txt = Join-Path $env:TEMP "$($paper.Name).txt"
  try {
    $response = Invoke-WebRequest -Uri $paper.Url -OutFile $pdf -PassThru -MaximumRedirection 8 -ErrorAction Stop
    $bytes = [System.IO.File]::ReadAllBytes($pdf)
    $magic = if ($bytes.Length -ge 4) { [Text.Encoding]::ASCII.GetString($bytes, 0, 4) } else { '' }
    "$($paper.Name): status=$($response.StatusCode) contentType=$($response.Headers.'Content-Type') bytes=$($bytes.Length) magic=$magic"
    if ($magic -eq '%PDF' -and $pdftotext) {
      & $pdftotext.Source -layout $pdf $txt
      "$($paper.Name): text=$txt lines=$((Get-Content $txt).Count)"
    }
  } catch {
    "$($paper.Name): ERROR $($_.Exception.Message)"
  }
}

  '^ros-jazzy-mujoco-ros2-control$',
  '^ros-jazzy-mujoco-vendor$',
  '^ros-jazzy-ros2-control$',
  '^ros-jazzy-ros2-controllers$',
  '^ros-jazzy-pinocchio$',
  '^ros-jazzy-crocoddyl$',
  '^ros-jazzy-tsid$',
  '^ros-jazzy-proxsuite$',
  '^ros-jazzy-osqp-vendor$',
  '^ros-jazzy-qpoases-vendor$',
  '^ros-jazzy-eigen-quadprog$',
  '^ros-jazzy-eigen-qld$',
  '^ros-jazzy-copra$'
)
$rows = foreach ($architecture in $architectures) {
  $uri = "https://packages.ros.org/ros2/ubuntu/dists/noble/main/binary-$architecture/Packages.gz"
  $response = Invoke-WebRequest -Uri $uri -UseBasicParsing
  $compressed = [System.IO.MemoryStream]::new([byte[]]$response.Content)
  $gzip = [System.IO.Compression.GZipStream]::new($compressed, [System.IO.Compression.CompressionMode]::Decompress)
  $reader = [System.IO.StreamReader]::new($gzip)
  $text = $reader.ReadToEnd()
  $reader.Dispose(); $gzip.Dispose(); $compressed.Dispose()
  foreach ($stanza in ($text -split "`n`n")) {
    $package = [regex]::Match($stanza, '(?m)^Package: (.+)$').Groups[1].Value.Trim()
    if ($package -and ($patterns | Where-Object { $package -match $_ })) {
      [pscustomobject]@{
        Index = $architecture
        Package = $package
        Version = [regex]::Match($stanza, '(?m)^Version: (.+)$').Groups[1].Value.Trim()
        Architecture = [regex]::Match($stanza, '(?m)^Architecture: (.+)$').Groups[1].Value.Trim()
        Filename = [regex]::Match($stanza, '(?m)^Filename: (.+)$').Groups[1].Value.Trim()
        SHA256 = [regex]::Match($stanza, '(?m)^SHA256: (.+)$').Groups[1].Value.Trim()
      }
    }
  }
}

Ran terminal command: function Get-CurlBytes([string]$uri) { $psi=[System.Diagnostics.ProcessStartInfo]::new(); $psi.FileName='curl.exe'; $psi.UseShellExecute=$false; $psi.RedirectStandardOutput=$true; $psi.RedirectStandardError=$true; [void]$psi.ArgumentList.Add('-fsSL'); [void]$psi.ArgumentList.Add($uri); $process=[System.Diagnostics.Process]::Start($psi); $memory=[System.IO.MemoryStream]::new(); $process.StandardOutput.BaseStream.CopyTo($memory); $errorText=$process.StandardError.ReadToEnd(); $process.WaitForExit(); if($process.ExitCode -ne 0){throw "curl failed: $errorText"}; $memory.ToArray() }; $base='https://mirrors.tuna.tsinghua.edu.cn/ros2/ubuntu/dists/noble'; $releaseBytes=Get-CurlBytes "$base/InRelease"; $releaseText=[System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8.GetString($releaseBytes); $date=[regex]::Match($releaseText,'(?m)^Date: (.+)$').Groups[1].Value.Trim(); Write-Output "InRelease Date: $date"; foreach($architecture in @('amd64','arm64')){ $relative="main/binary-$architecture/Packages.gz"; $bytes=Get-CurlBytes "$base/$relative"; $actual=([System.BitConverter]::ToString([System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256]::HashData($bytes))).Replace('-','').ToLowerInvariant(); $line=[regex]::Match($releaseText,"(?m)^ ([0-9a-f]{64})\s+(\d+)\s+$([regex]::Escape($relative))$"); if(-not $line.Success){throw "No signed SHA256 entry for $relative"}; $expected=$line.Groups[1].Value; Write-Output "$architecture bytes=$($bytes.Length) sha256=$actual signedMatch=$($actual -eq $expected)" }

**1. Model Authority**
`robot_model.yaml` owns typed physical parameters and identities; Xacro owns composition without duplicated numeric values. Hashed CAD assets and identification datasets are also authored inputs. A deterministic model compiler emits resolved URDF, `ros2_control` configuration, C++ joint maps, policy-I/O schemas, simulator models, and a safety manifest containing model, calibration, actuator-map, and wire-scaling identities.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    A["Schema model + Xacro composition"] --> G["Validated model compiler"]
    D["CAD + identification datasets"] --> G
    G --> U["Resolved URDF + ros2_control"]
    G --> P["Controller and policy schemas"]
    G --> S["ADR-002 safety manifest"]
    G --> M["MJCF + MuJoCo overlay"]
    G --> I["USD + Isaac overlay"]
```

| Gate | Measurement and acceptance | Permitted motion |
|---|---|---|
| G0 Topology | Fix 14-joint tree, motors, mechanisms, height/mass/battery envelope and sensor inventory | Drives disabled; component benches only |
| G1 Kinematics | Survey axes, signs, ratios, offsets and limits; 30 held-out poses; end-effector RMS $\le0.5\%$ leg length, max $\le1\%$, angular RMS $\le0.5^\circ$ | Supported, low-energy keyframes |
| G2 Mass geometry | Weigh assemblies; suspension/pendulum tests; total mass error $\le1\%$, segment mass $\le3\%$, CoM error $\le1\%$ leg length, inertia-period error $\le5\%$ | Quasi-static stand on rig |
| G3 Actuators | Dynamometer torque-current-speed, friction, backlash, compliance, delay and thermal derating; held-out torque error $\le10\%$, delay error $\le1$ ms | Supported joint excitation |
| G4 Contacts | Identify turf friction, sole compliance, CoP/force sensing and impacts; contact timing error $\le5$ ms and force/impulse error $\le10\%$ | Tethered dynamic stepping |
| G5 Correlation | 100 held-out motions; joint/base NRMSE $\le10\%$, contact timing $\le10$ ms, current/energy error $\le15\%$ | Staged untethered locomotion |

**2. Execution And Downselect**
Adopt a mandatory analytical commissioning gait, then a primary closed-loop LIPM/DCM generator with Pinocchio/TSID WBC. Permit exactly one challenger: initially asynchronous centroidal MPC. A learned reference or hybrid controller may replace, but not join, that challenger slot after G5.

```mermaid
flowchart TB
    Z["Zenoh / ROS intent"] --> N["Non-RT ingress and validation"]
    N --> C["Primary or one challenger"]
    C --> B["Per-source bounded SPSC reference buffer"]
    B --> R["200 Hz controller: state, primitive, realization, tuple"]
    R --> S["SafetyKernel"]
    S --> H["HumanoidActuatorSystem"]
    H --> T["STM32, MuJoCo, or Isaac transport"]
    R -. copied snapshot .-> E["Non-RT logging and evaluator"]
```

Every candidate implements the same fixed-capacity state/reference/primitive contracts, including sequence, monotonic timestamps, model/calibration IDs, availability epoch, contact mode, execution time, validity horizon, solver status, constraint margin and artifact ID.

Hard safety and deadline gates precede scoring. Qualified candidates are scored: push robustness 25%, directional speed/tracking 15%, kick quality 15%, get-up success 15%, P0 compute headroom 15%, sim-to-real gap 10%, reproducibility/maintenance 5%. Require total $\ge75$, every category $\ge60$, and a five-point advantage with 95% confidence; otherwise retain the primary.

Use 1,000 held-out SIL seeds, 100 tethered command bouts, 30 trials per push/kick/get-up condition, and 20 untethered match-duration runs. Normalize speed by $\sqrt{gL}$ and impulse by $m\sqrt{gL}$. Downselect at the earliest of 40 challenger engineer-days, eight calendar weeks after G4/SIL readiness, or 16 weeks before competition. The losing implementation becomes replay-only test material.

**3. Simulator Contract**
Isaac Lab 2.3.2 on an x86_64 RTX workstation owns parallel training and domain randomization. MuJoCo 3.4.0 owns lightweight controller SIL, deterministic CI regression and the independent dynamics/contact comparison.

`MujocoActuatorTransport` and `IsaacActuatorTransport` must pass ADR-001’s transport-conformance suite: identical units, signs, ordering, sequence/freshness, reset behavior, fault injection and atomic five-field snapshots. The same compiled controller loads unchanged against simulation or STM32 transport. USD/MJCF overlays may hold simulator numerics, never independent physical parameters.

The native runtime plane is provisionally pinned to `ros2_control` 4.45.2, Pinocchio 4.0.0, TSID 1.10.0 and ProxSuite 0.6.5. Crocoddyl and BLF remain isolated challenger processes; apt and conda dynamics libraries cannot coexist in one process.

**4. Real-Time Boundary**
Use one synchronous custom `ros2_control` controller in Controller Manager’s existing RT thread. Reject a second RT thread and chained final controllers: they add scheduling and ownership boundaries without improving the fixed 5 ms exchange.

Zenoh/ROS callbacks run on non-RT executors. They validate and copy fixed-size references into per-producer lock-free SPSC buffers. The RT loop never accesses middleware or shared-memory IPC. Plans arrive at least 10 ms before first execution, contain at least two update periods plus 10 ms of validity, and expire into a qualified stop rather than indefinite reuse.

**5. MIT Mapping**
$$
\tau_j=K_{p,j}(q_{d,j}-q_j)+K_{d,j}(\dot q_{d,j}-\dot q_j)+\tau_{ff,j}
$$

| Candidate | Tuple allocation |
|---|---|
| Keyframe/LIPM | Sampler owns $q_d,\dot q_d$; initially $\tau_{ff}=0$; manifest-selected phase/joint gain schedule |
| WBC/MPC | WBC owns model feed-forward; either net torque with zero gains or feed-forward-only torque with separately allocated drive impedance |
| Learned reference | Policy supplies bounded references; owner fills every joint, approved gains and zero/model torque exactly as represented during training |
| Residual | One combiner computes $\operatorname{sat}(\tau_{model}+\alpha\Delta\tau)$ once; learned gains are excluded initially |

Feedback may appear through $\tau_{ff}$ or drive impedance, never both. The tuple owner initializes all five fields for all joints every cycle before coupled ADR-002 validation. Use C++20 for RT, deployed adapters and production inference; Python is limited to training, analysis and tooling.

**6. Primitives And Degradation**
The qualified graph is `SAFE_HOLD -> STAND -> WALK <-> KICK`; any nominal primitive may enter `CONTROLLED_STOP`; a fall preempts into `BRACE -> POST_FALL -> GET_UP/SELF_RIGHT -> SAFE_HOLD`. Rearming interlocks still apply before renewed active play.

Each primitive declares version, model/calibration IDs, initial-state envelope, required exact availability mask/epoch, contacts, gain profile, maximum duration, completion predicate, abort target and safety-policy ID. Balance arm swing belongs to stand/walk; bracing belongs to fall handling; goalkeeper motions remain outside Topic 3.

A degraded-policy manifest must match exact fault class, joint bitmap, posture/contact mode, model/calibration IDs and availability epoch. Isolation is verified first, remaining joints damp, the controller acknowledges the new epoch and exact policy ID, and `SafetyCoordinator` authorizes execution. Initially, a qualified single-arm loss may permit controlled stop and stand only; any leg, estimator, IMU or contact-sensing loss resolves to damping then disable. No wildcard or “closest policy” matching is allowed.

**7. Compute Profiles**
The maximum design allocation on every profile is: feedback/read 0.35 ms, estimation/contact guard 0.45 ms, primitive/reference selection 0.15 ms, candidate realization 1.35 ms, tuple safety 0.35 ms and encoding/write handoff 0.35 ms. This totals 3.0 ms and reserves 2.0 ms for measured scheduler, physical-I/O and jitter margin. Any rebudget must preserve the 5 ms maximum and ADR-002 reaction deadlines.

| Profile | Admitted workload |
|---|---|
| P0 Orin Nano 8 GB | Primary LIPM/WBC and at most one compact reference-policy experiment |
| P1 Orin NX 16 GB | Concurrent centroidal MPC or hybrid challenger |
| P2 AGX Orin | Whole-body optimization, larger trackers and qualification instrumentation |

Reserve one isolated CPU core for the RT owner and another core/IRQ allocation for transport; prefault and lock memory; keep GPU work outside the hard path. Asynchronous solve maximum must remain below half its update period. Promotion requires eight-hour stressed/thermal tests, zero 5 ms misses, and at least 40% headroom in every assigned CPU, GPU and memory partition. Topic 9 will formalize capability detection, power modes and profile manifests.

This proposal is ready for acceptance or targeted amendments. After acceptance, I will checkpoint it as ADR-004, or a small ADR set if the model-governance and locomotion-runtime concerns warrant separation, then update `DECISIONS.md` and `ARCHITECTURE.md`.

Made changes.
